6
Relationships with affine Lie algebras

6.1
Affine Lie algebras

In this section, we review basic concepts of affine Lie algebras and their integrable highest weight modules. See
[
14
]
for more detail. Let
X_{r}
(
=A_{r}
,
B_{r}
,
C_{r}
,
D_{r}
,
E_{6,7,8}
,
F_{4}
or
G_{2}
) be a finite type Dynkin diagram, and
\mathfrak{g}
be the finite dimensional simple Lie algebra of type
X_{r}
over
\mathbb{C}
. Let
\mathfrak{h}
be a Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
, and
\Delta
be the set of roots. We also use the notations on root systems that we used in Section
3.1
.
We extend the inner product
(\cdot\,|\,\cdot)
to the nondegenerate symmetric bilinear form on
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
. We define the following two free abelian groups:

\displaystyle Q=\bigoplus_{a=1}^{r}\mathbb{Z}\alpha_{a},\qquad M=\bigoplus_{a=1}^{r}\mathbb{Z}t_{a}\alpha_{a}.

We also define the following sets:

\displaystyle P=\left\{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}\,\bigg|\,\frac{2(\Lambda\,|\,\alpha_{a})}{(\alpha_{a}\,|\,\alpha_{a})}\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all $a=1,\dots,r$}\right\},

\displaystyle P_{+}=\left\{\Lambda\in P\,\bigg|\,\frac{2(\Lambda\,|\,\alpha_{a})}{(\alpha_{a}\,|\,\alpha_{a})}\geq 0\text{ for all $a=1,\dots,r$}\right\}.

Let

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathbb{C}\big[t,t^{-1}\big]\oplus\mathbb{C}K\oplus\mathbb{C}d

be the affine Lie algebra associated with
\mathfrak{g}
. The Lie bracket on
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
is defined as

\displaystyle\big[X\otimes t^{m},Y\otimes t^{n}\big]=[X,Y]\otimes t^{m+n}+m\delta_{m+n,0}\cdot(X\,|\,Y)K,

\displaystyle[K,\hat{\mathfrak{g}}]=\{0\},

\displaystyle\big[d,X\otimes t^{n}\big]=nX\otimes t^{n}.

Fix a non-negative integer
\ell
. Let us define the following set:

\displaystyle P_{+}^{\ell}=\{\Lambda\in P_{+}\,|\,(\Lambda\,|\,\theta)\leq\ell\},

where
\theta
is the highest root in
\Delta
. For any
\Lambda\in P_{+}^{\ell}
, there is the unique level
\ell
integrable highest weight
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
-module such that the classical part of its highest weight is
\Lambda
, which is denoted by
L(\Lambda)
.

We define the following rational numbers associated with
L(\Lambda)
:

\displaystyle c(\ell)=\frac{\ell\dim\mathfrak{g}}{\ell+h^{\vee}},\qquad h_{\Lambda}=\frac{(\Lambda\,|\,\Lambda+2\rho)}{2(\ell+h^{\vee})}.

Let
q={\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\tau}
. For any diagonalizable linear map
\alpha\colon V\to V
with eigenvalues
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dots
with finite multiplicities
m_{1},m_{2},\dots
, we define the trace
\mathrm{tr}_{V}q^{\alpha}
by
\mathrm{tr}_{V}q^{\alpha}=\sum_{i}m_{i}q^{\lambda_{i}}
.
We define the following two functions as in
[
16
]
:

\displaystyle\chi_{\Lambda}(\tau)=q^{-\frac{c(\ell)}{24}}\tr_{L(\Lambda)}q^{h_{\Lambda}-d},\qquad b_{\lambda}^{\Lambda}(\tau)=q^{-\frac{c(\ell)-r}{24}}\tr_{U(\Lambda,\lambda)}q^{h_{\Lambda}-\frac{(\lambda\,|\,\lambda)}{2\ell}-d},

(6.1)

where
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
and

\displaystyle U(\Lambda,\lambda)=\big\{v\in L(\Lambda)\,|\,\big(h\otimes t^{n}\big)v=\delta_{n,0}\lambda(h)v\text{ for all $h\in\mathfrak{h},n\geq 0$}\big\}.

These series converge to holomorphic functions on the upper half-plane
\mathbb{H}=\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}\,|\,\image\tau>0\}
.
The function
\chi_{\Lambda}(\tau)
is called the (specialized)
normalized character
2
2

2

More precisely,
\chi_{\Lambda}(\tau)
is the normalized character in
[
16
]
at
z=t=0
.
of
L(\Lambda)
, and
b_{\lambda}^{\Lambda}(\tau)
is called the
branching function
for the pair
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h})
. We will simply call
b_{\lambda}^{\Lambda}(\tau)
as the branching function.
Note that dividing this branching function by the
r
-th power of the Dedekind eta function yields the
string function
in
[
15
]
.

The asymptotics of the normalized character and the branching function were studied in detailed in
[
15
,
16
]
. Let
\tau\downarrow 0
denote the limit in the positive imaginary axis.

Theorem 6.1

(
[
15
,
16
]
)
.

Suppose that
\lambda\in\Lambda+Q
. Then

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\downarrow 0}\chi_{\Lambda}(\tau){\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}c(\ell)}{12\tau}}=a(\Lambda),

(6.2)

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\downarrow 0}b_{\lambda}^{\Lambda}(\tau){\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}(c(\ell)-r)}{12\tau}}=\lvert P/Q\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}\lvert Q/\ell M\rvert^{-\frac{1}{2}}a(\Lambda),

(6.3)

where
a(\Lambda)
is the real number defined by

\displaystyle a(\Lambda)=\big\lvert P/\big(\ell+h^{\vee}\big)M\big\rvert^{-\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_{+}}2\sin\frac{\pi(\Lambda+\rho\,|\,\alpha)}{\ell+h^{\vee}}.

The real number
a(\Lambda)
is called the
asymptotic dimension
of
L(\Lambda)
for the following reason.
The module
L(\Lambda)
is infinite dimensional unless
\Lambda
is trivial, and
\chi_{\Lambda}(0)=\dim L(\Lambda)=\infty
.
However, (
6.2
) says that by multiplying by the appropriate exponential term and taking the limit, we can get the finite number
a(\Lambda)
.
Therefore, we can think that the real number
a(\Lambda)
is the “dimension” of
L(\Lambda)
.

6.2
Exponents and asymptotic dimension

The branching functions with
\Lambda=0
are expected to coincide with the partition
q
-series that we studied in Section
5.4
via the conjectural formula of the branching functions in
[
22
]
.
This was first observed in
[
17
]
for the total partition
q
-series with
X=ADE
.
Let
b_{\lambda}^{\Lambda}(q)
denote the formal
q
-series defined by the right-hand side of (
6.1
).

Conjecture 6.2

(
[
22
]
)
.

Suppose that
\ell\geq 2
.
Let
\gamma=\gamma(X_{r},\ell)
be the mutation loop defined in Section
3.3
. Suppose that
\sigma\in H_{\gamma}
, and
\lambda\in Q
is a representative of
F(\sigma)
, where
F
is defined as (
5.5
). Then

\displaystyle q^{-\frac{c(\ell)-r}{24}}\mathcal{Z}_{\gamma}^{\sigma}(q)=b_{\lambda}^{0}(q).

(6.4)

By summing (
6.4
) for all elements in
Q/\ell M
, we obtain the following conjectural identity on the total partition
q
-series:

\displaystyle q^{-\frac{c(\ell)-r}{24}}\mathcal{Z}_{\gamma}(q)=\sum_{\lambda\in Q/\ell M}b_{\lambda}^{0}(q).

(6.5)

By comparing the asymptotics (
5.4
) and (
6.3
),
we find that (
6.5
) yields the following identities:

\displaystyle\frac{6}{\pi^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}L\big(z_{+}(\eta;t)\big)=c(\ell)-r,

(6.6)

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{I-J_{\gamma}(\eta)}}=\lvert P/Q\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}a(0),

(6.7)

where we use
\det A_{+}=\lvert Q/\ell M\rvert
, which follows from Proposition
5.15
.

The identity (
6.6
) is called the dilogarithm identity in conformal field theories, and proved in
[
12
,
13
,
29
]
by using cluster algebras. On the other hand, the identity (
6.7
) is exactly Conjecture
3.8
at
x=1
because

\displaystyle\frac{N_{X_{r},\ell}(1)}{D_{X_{r},\ell}(1)}

\displaystyle=\prod_{a=1}^{r}t_{a}\big(\ell+h^{\vee}\big){\left({\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_{+}}2\sin\frac{\pi(\rho\,|\,\alpha)}{\ell+h^{\vee}}}\right)^{-2}}

\displaystyle={\big\lvert Q/\big(\ell+h^{\vee}\big)M\big\rvert}{\left({\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_{+}}2\sin\frac{\pi(\rho\,|\,\alpha)}{\ell+h^{\vee}}}\right)^{-2}}=\lvert P/Q\rvert^{-1}a(0)^{-2}.

Consequently, the conjectural identity (
6.7
) gives us a consistency between our conjecture on exponents (Conjecture
3.8
) and the known conjecture on
q
-series (Conjecture
6.2
), and also gives an interesting connection between the theory of cluster algebras and the representation theory of affine Lie algebras.
